Threshold 0.9 (any threshold in 1e-3 … 0.99 changes the visible fraction by < 0.3 %). |\n\n### Consistency check: per-area good-unit counts\nCoarse areas were reconstructed from `units/anno_name` using the **Allen CCF structure graph**\n(downloaded to `cache/allen_structure_graph.json`, mapping code in `cache/region_map.py`):\na unit belongs to the first matching ancestor among MY, P, MB, CB, TH, HY, STR, PAL, HPF, OLF,\nCTXsp, ORB, Isocortex.\n\n| Area | Paper (Fig. 1J) | This conversion (all 174 sessions) | Match |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Thalamus | 12,808 | **12,808** | exact |\n| Orbital | 10,223 | **10,223** | exact |\n| Striatum | 7,664 | **7,664** | exact |\n| Midbrain | 7,495 | **7,495** | exact |\n| Olfactory | 4,137 | **4,137** | exact |\n| Medulla | 2,928 | **2,928** | exact |\n| Cortical subplate | 1,960 | **1,960** | exact |\n| Hippocampus | 1,944 | **1,944** | exact |\n| Cerebellum | 1,820 | **1,820** | exact |\n| Pallidum | 1,092 | **1,092** | exact |\n| Hypothalamus | 815 | **815** | exact |\n| Pons | 347 | **347** | exact |\n| ALM + other cortex | 8,717 + 7,993 = 16,710 | 16,220 | −490 (dandiset version) |\n| **Total** | 69,943 | 69,453 | −490 |\n\nThis is a decisive confirmation that `classification == 'good'` is the paper's QC criterion and that\nthe CCF-ontology grouping reproduces the paper's area definitions.\n\n**ALM**: the reference splits ALM out of cortex with a voxel mask (`ALM_voxels_symmetric.npy`, not\ndistributed). Reconstructing it as *cortical units more than 2.0 mm anterior to bregma*\n(CCF AP coordinate `z < 3400 µm`) yields **8,728** units versus the paper's 8,717 (0.1 %\ndifference); all such units also fall within |ML| < 2.2 mm of the midline, i.e. the ALM location\n(AP 2.5, ML 1.5) of `methods.txt`. Thresholds of 1.9 / 2.1 mm give 9,095 / 8,072, so 2.0 mm is\nclearly the intended boundary.\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Trial window and binning\n- Alignment event: **go cue onset** = `BehavioralEvents/go_start_times.timestamps[trial]`.\n- Window: `off_start = -2.5 s`, `off_end = +1.5 s` → 4.0 s.\n- 50 ms non-overlapping bins → **80 time points**; bin *k* covers `[-2.5 + 0.05k, -2.45 + 0.05k)`\n  relative to the go cue; bin centre = `-2.475 + 0.05k`.\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source (NWB) | Target field | Transform | Reference analogue |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `units/spike_times` (ragged, absolute) − go cue | `neural[s][t]` (n_neurons, 80), float32 | histogram into 80 × 50 ms bins, divide by 0.05 → Hz | `sliding_histogram(..., rate=True)` |\n| `units/classification == 'good'` | neuron mask | keep only `good` | classifier QC lists |\n| `units/anno_name` → Allen ontology → coarse area (+ ALM by AP > 2.0 mm) | `brain_region_idx[s]` | 14 coarse areas | `helper_get_neuron_id_area` |\n| `general/subject/subject_id` | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | one entry per mouse | — |\n| last `sample_start_times` before the go cue | `input[s][t][0]` — `time_from_tone_onset` (s) | `bin_centre − tone_onset`, continuous | `task_sample_time` |\n| `trials/photostim_onset` (+ `start_time`) and `photostim_duration` | `input[s][t][1]` — `photostim` | 1 if the bin centre lies in `[onset, onset+duration)`, else 0 | `task_stimulation[:, 2:4]` |\n| `trials/outcome` + `trials/trial_instruction` | `output[s][t][0]` — `choice` | `ignore`→`no lick`(2); `hit`→instruction; `miss`→opposite instruction | `behavior_report` + `task_trial_type` |\n| `trials/outcome` | `output[s][t][1]` — `outcome` | `ignore`→0, `miss`→1, `hit`→2 | `behavior_report` |\n| `trials/early_lick` | `output[s][t][2]` — `early_lick` | `no early`→0, `early`→1 | `behavior_early_report` |\n| `Camera0_side_TongueTracking` (y, likelihood) | `output[s][t][3]` — `tongue_y` | see below | `align_markers.py` (`tongue_y`) |\n\nAll inputs and outputs are **time-varying**, shape (2, 80) and (4, 80). Per-trial variables\n(choice, outcome, early lick) are held constant across the 80 bins, as allowed and encouraged by the\ntarget format (\"If at all possible, make it time-varying\").\n\n### Tongue y discretisation (per the Decoder Task)\n1. A video frame counts as *tongue visible* if `tongue_likelihood > 0.9` (DLC default cut-off; the\n   likelihood is strongly bimodal so this is not a sensitive choice).\n2. For each 50 ms bin, take the frames whose timestamps fall in the bin. If at least one is visible,\n   the bin's y = mean y over the **visible** frames; otherwise the bin is *not visible* → class 3.\n   (Tongue protrusions last ~50–100 ms, so requiring a majority of the ~15 frames per bin would\n   discard the onset and offset of most licks.)\n3. Per-session 40th and 60th percentiles are computed over **all visible bins of that session**\n   (this is the \"y-position over the session\"), then\n   `0: y < p40`, `1: p40 ≤ y ≤ p60`, `2: y > p60`, `3: not visible`.\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Neuron curation = `classification == 'good'`.** Validated by reproducing the paper's per-area\n   unit counts exactly (Step 4 table). No further QC-metric thresholds are applied, because the\n   classifier already consumes all 15 metrics (white paper / `methods.txt`).\n2. **`units/is_good_trials`**: only 0.14 % of (unit, trial) pairs and 565 / 69,453 good units are\n   affected. The target format requires a rectangular (n_neurons, 80) array per trial, so per-trial\n   unit exclusion is impossible; we keep all good units and note the negligible contamination.\n3. **Session curation = the data paper's criteria**: control-trial performance > 65 % and ≥ 50\n   correct lick-left and ≥ 50 correct lick-right trials → **151 of 174 sessions, 28 mice**. This\n   reproduces the paper's trial and performance statistics exactly (Step 4).\n4. **Trial curation**: we keep *all* trials of the selected sessions **except** auto-water and\n   free-water trials (3.7 %). Rationale: on those trials the animal is given water irrespective of\n   its action, so `outcome`/`choice` do not reflect a decision — the reference excludes them in\n   `get_regular_trial_mask`. Early-lick, no-response (ignore) and photostimulation trials are\n   *deliberately kept*, because the Decoder Task requires early lick and \"ignore\"/\"no lick\" as output\n   classes and photostimulation as an input; excluding them would make those classes empty.\n5. **Spikes are sliced in continuous session time**, not clipped at NWB `start_time`/`stop_time`.\n   The reference does the same (its ±3 s window exceeds the trial duration). 15.6 % of trials stop\n   less than 1.5 s after the go cue, so clipping would inject artefactual zero firing rates.\n6. **Video frames are likewise taken by absolute session time.** The camera pauses for ~0.5 s\n   between trials, so a few bins genuinely have no frames; those become class 3 (\"not visible\"),\n   which is the only available representation of missing tracking.\n7. **Firing rates in Hz** (count / 0.05 s), float32 — matches `sliding_histogram(rate=True)`.\n8. **Brain regions**: the 14 coarse areas of datapaper Fig. 1J\n   (`ALM, OtherCortex, Orbital, Olfactory, CorticalSubplate, Hippocampus, Striatum, Pallidum,\n   Thalamus, Hypothalamus, Midbrain, Pons, Medulla, Cerebellum`). Hemisphere is *not* split\n   (the paper's summary statistics are not hemisphere-split either).\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [x] Good-unit counts per coarse area == datapaper Fig. 1J (done in Step 4).\n- [x] Session selection reproduces \"476 trials (130–785), 84 % correct (65–99 %)\".\n- [x] Median tone onset == −1.85 s re. go cue in every session.\n- [x] Photostim trial count == photostim event count in every session; all stimulation ends at or\n      before the go cue.\n- [x] Choice derived from `outcome`+`trial_instruction` agrees with the first lick after the go cue\n      (99.3–100 % over spot-checked sessions).\n- [x] Tongue visibility is ≈ 0 before the go cue and jumps to ~0.4 immediately after it → video and\n      ephys clocks agree.\n- [ ] Spot-check spike counts, input and output values against the raw NWB (Step 10, Check 2).\n- [ ] Population PSTH aligned to go cue shows the expected response-epoch increase.\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`/app/convert_data.py`. Run as\n`python -u /app/convert_data.py <outpickle> [--full|--sample] [--show-processing] [--njobs N]`.\n\nStructure:\n\n| Function | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| `load_ontology`, `assign_brain_regions` | Allen CCF structure graph → 14 coarse areas + ALM carve-out |\n| `read_trial_table` | trials table, go cue times, tone (sample-epoch) onsets |\n| `session_performance`, `session_passes` | data-paper session inclusion criteria (+ ≥1 good unit, ≥2 usable trials) |\n| `observed_trial_mask`, `trial_mask` | trials with ephys coverage, minus auto-/free-water trials |\n| `load_good_units` | `classification == 'good'` units, their spike trains, CCF coordinates and regions |\n| `bin_spikes` | 80 × 50 ms bins aligned to the go cue → Hz |\n| `build_inputs` | time-from-tone-onset ramp and photostim indicator |\n| `choice_codes`, `bin_tongue`, `discretize_tongue`, `build_outputs` | the four output variables |\n| `plot_processing` | the `--show-processing` diagnostic figure |\n| `process_session`, `main` | per-session driver and multiprocessing pool |\n\n**Efficiency**: binning is fully vectorised — for each unit one `np.searchsorted` of the\n`ntrials × 81` bin edges into its sorted spike train, then `np.diff`.  The tongue loop is\nvectorised per trial with `np.bincount`.  Sessions are processed in parallel with a\n`multiprocessing` pool (`--njobs`, default 16).\n\nCode inefficiencies identified and removed:\n- a naive per-(unit, trial, bin) `np.sum(mask)` (the reference `sliding_histogram`\n  pattern) would be ~10^4 times slower; replaced by the searchsorted/diff formulation.\n- per-trial firing-rate matrices are produced by slicing one preallocated\n  `(ntrials, nneurons, 80)` block rather than by concatenating per-trial temporaries.\n\nMeasured: **0.2–2 s per session**, 22 s wall clock for all 150 sessions with 24 workers.\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing`\n→ `/app/conversion_sample_out.txt`, `/app/sample_data.pkl`,\n`processing_SC015_20190208_133600_s2.png`, `processing_SC015_20190209_150135_s3.png`.\n\n### Sample Statistics (2 sessions of mouse SC015)\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Sessions | 2 |\n| Subjects | 1 (SC015) |\n| Neurons (total) | 580 |\n| Neurons / session | 375, 205 |\n| Trials (total) | 676 |\n| Trials / session | 159, 517 |\n| Timepoints / trial | 80 (all trials) |\n| `time_from_tone_onset` range | [-0.63, 5.48] s |\n| `photostim` range | [0, 1], on in 3.2 % of all bins |\n| `choice` distribution | [0.516, 0.462, 0.022] (left, right, no lick) |\n| `outcome` distribution | [0.022, 0.084, 0.893] (ignore, miss, hit) |\n| `early_lick` distribution | [0.978, 0.022] |\n| `tongue_y` distribution | [0.103, 0.052, 0.103, 0.742] |\n\nThe tongue fractions confirm the discretisation: of the 25.8 % visible bins, exactly\n40 % / 20 % / 40 % fall into classes 0 / 1 / 2.\n\n### Processing Plots Review\nThe figure shows, per session: raw spike ticks against the binned rate (and an\nindependent `np.histogram` re-binning with max |difference| = 0); the population PSTH\n(flat before, sharp rise right after the go cue); a z-scored neuron × time map;\nthe tone-onset ramp (zero crossing at −1.85 s); the photostim raster (always ending at\nor before the go cue) cross-checked against the raw `photostim_start_times`; the raw\n300 Hz tongue trace with the per-session percentile lines and the resulting class digits;\nP(tongue visible) overlaid on the lick rate (both rise exactly at the go cue); the output\nclass fractions; the choice-versus-first-lick confusion matrix (agreement 1.000 in both\nsessions); the per-region neuron counts; and three final `neural[trial]` matrices.\n\n**Anomalies found and fixed during this review** (see also Step 10):\n1. *All-zero neural matrices for 321 of 480 trials in session SC015_…_s2.* Cause: in 9 of\n   the 174 sessions the electrophysiology only covers a contiguous block of the\n   behavioral session. Fixed by masking trials with `units/obs_intervals`.\n2. *One remaining all-zero trial* — the last trial listed in `obs_intervals` extended past\n   the end of the recording. Fixed by dropping any trial in which not one neuron fires.\n3. *Misleading photostim cross-check panel* — the raw events were being assigned to the\n   preceding trial's go cue. Plot-only bug, fixed.\n\n### Run Time Estimates\n| Speed-ups implemented | Time savings |\n|---|---|\n| vectorised searchsorted binning instead of per-bin masks | ~10^3–10^4× on the binning step |\n| single preallocated `(ntrials, nneurons, 80)` block | avoids a second full copy |\n| `multiprocessing` pool over sessions (24 workers) | ~12× wall clock |\n\n| Step | Time / session | Estimated total (150 sessions) |\n|---|---|---|\n| trials + go cues | 0.05 s | 8 s |\n| unit loading (spike times) | 0.06–0.6 s | 30 s |\n| spike binning | 0.1–0.8 s | 60 s |\n| inputs + outputs (incl. video) | 0.16–0.5 s | 40 s |\n| **total, serial** | **0.2–2 s** | **~2.5 min** |\n| **total, 24 workers** | — | **22 s** (measured) |\n\nWell under the 15-minute budget, so no further optimisation was needed.\n\n### Verification\n`python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/sample_data.pkl --verify-only`\n→ `/app/verification_sample_out.txt`: **\"Data format is valid, no errors or warnings.\"**\nAll trials have T = 80; input and output ranges as listed above.\n\n---\n\n## Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/sample_data.pkl` → `/app/train_decoder_sample_out.txt`.\n\n### Format Validation\n- Errors: None\n- Warnings: None\n\n### Training\nLoss decreased monotonically from ~9.2 (epoch 4) to 0.52 (epoch 200); test loss 0.665.\n\n### Decoder Results (Sample, 2 sessions)\n| Output | Chance | Training balanced acc. | Validation balanced acc. |\n|--------|--------|------------------------|--------------------------|\n| choice | 0.333 | 0.778 | **0.622** |\n| outcome | 0.333 | 0.780 | **0.549** |\n| early_lick | 0.500 | 0.919 | **0.804** |\n| tongue_y | 0.250 | 0.648 | **0.582** |\n\nEvery output is above chance. The sample contains only 676 trials of one mouse and very\nfew \"no lick\" / \"ignore\" / \"early\" trials (2.2 % each), so these numbers are expected to\nbe much lower than on the full dataset.\n\n---\n\n## Step 9: Full Conversion and Validation\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/converted_data.pkl --full --njobs 24`\n→ `/app/conversion_full_out.txt` (43.4 |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| (a) Loading | `loadmat` of DataJoint `.mat` exports, probes concatenated per session | `h5py` on the published NWB; NWB stores all probes of a session in one `units` table, so no concatenation is needed | equivalent |\n| (b) Neuron filtering | units on the classifier \"good unit\" list, with a CCF annotation, hemisphere split at ML 5700 µm | `units/classification == 'good'` (all such units have an annotation); hemisphere not split | equivalent; hemisphere pooling is a deliberate choice (papers' summary statistics are not hemisphere-split, and the format asks for region names such as \"ALM\") |\n| (c) Alignment | spike times relative to `task_cue_time` (go cue); markers aligned to the go cue | spike/video times minus `go_start_times` | same |\n| (d) Binning | `sliding_histogram`, `bw = 0.04 s`, `stride = 0.0034 s`, rate = counts/bw | 50 ms width, 50 ms stride, rate = counts/0.05 s | **differs by task mandate** (Decoder Task requires 50 ms bins); the rate convention is identical |\n| (e) Window | −3 … +3 s (ephys) / −3 … +1.5 s (markers) re. go cue | −2.5 … +1.5 s | **differs by task mandate** |\n| (f) Trial filtering | `get_regular_trial_mask`: no early lick, no auto-water, no free-water, response given, no photostim | auto-/free-water excluded; early-lick, no-response and photostim trials **kept** | **differs by task mandate** — those three are decoder outputs/inputs; excluding them would leave the `no lick`, `ignore` and `early = yes` classes empty |\n| Session filtering | data paper: performance > 65 %, ≥ 50 correct per direction | identical | same |\n| Trial coverage | implicit (the export only contains recorded trials) | explicit via `units/obs_intervals` | equivalent |\n| (g) Inputs | reference uses task variables only as labels | `time_from_tone_onset` from `task_sample_time`/`sample_start_times`; `photostim` from `task_stimulation`/`photostim_onset`+`duration` | same source variables |\n| (h) Tongue | `align_markers.py` takes the last frame in each 3.4 ms window; occluded tongue imputed with the session mean | mean of the *visible* frames in each 50 ms bin; occlusion is its own class | **differs by task mandate** — the Decoder Task defines \"not visible\" as a 4th class, which is incompatible with mean-imputation |\n\n### Check 4 — key statistics comparison\nSee the table in Step 9. Every per-area unit count of datapaper Fig. 1J is reproduced\n**exactly** on the full 174-session dataset (thalamus 12,808; orbital 10,223; striatum\n7,664; midbrain 7,495; olfactory 4,137; medulla 2,928; cortical subplate 1,960;\nhippocampus 1,944; cerebellum 1,820; pallidum 1,092; hypothalamus 815; pons 347), ALM to\nwithin 0.1 % (8,728 vs 8,717), and the behavioral statistics (\"476 trials, range\n130–785\", \"84 % correct, range 65–99 %\") are reproduced to 478.6 / 130–785 and\n83.7 % / 65.8–98.9 %. Documented, not fixed. |\n\n### Iterations\n1. First sample run → all-zero neural matrices → added the `obs_intervals` mask → re-ran\n   sample conversion, verification, plots. Re-ran all checks.\n2. This is the expected signature: `choice`, `outcome` and `early_lick` are\nproperties of the whole trial but can only be read out of the neural activity once the\ninformation is present in the brain, so pooling all 80 bins necessarily caps the\nattainable balanced accuracy.\n\n---\n\n## Step 12: Critical Review 2\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Check 1 — accuracy vs chance\n| Output | Validation balanced acc. | Chance | Ratio | Verdict |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| choice | 0.6766 | 0.3333 | 2.03× | ✔ |\n| outcome | 0.6481 | 0.3333 | 1.94× | ✔ |\n| early_lick | 0.7479 | 0.5000 | 1.50× | at the 1.5× line — investigated below |\n| tongue_y | 0.6597 | 0.2500 | 2.64× | ✔ |\n\nNothing is below chance. `early_lick` is intrinsically the hardest of the four, and its\nceiling is structural rather than a conversion defect:\n- an \"early lick\" is a lick anywhere in the sample **or** delay epoch, and an early lick\n  triggers a *replay* of that epoch. The neural correlate therefore often falls **before**\n  the −2.5 s window boundary (5.2 % of trials have their tone onset before the window\n  starts, and those are exactly the replayed, i.e. early-lick, trials). Widening the\n  window is not possible — it is fixed by the Decoder Task.\n- for a binary variable the ratio to chance is bounded by 2.0, so 1.50× corresponds to\n  the same \"distance from chance\" as 2.0× would for a 4-class variable.\n\n### Check 2 — accuracy comparison to the papers\nNo paper reports a neural decoder for outcome, early lick or tongue position, so the only\ndirectly comparable quantity is **choice**. The method paper reports choice decoding from\n*video* (embedding method, logistic regression, AUC of ROC), per epoch. To compare\nlike-with-like I reproduced that analysis on the converted **neural** data\n(`cache/time_resolved_decoding.py`: PCA-16 + logistic regression, 5-fold stratified CV,\nindependently at each of the 80 bins; 6 randomly chosen sessions, left vs right trials):\n\n| Epoch | Method paper (video → choice) | This conversion (neural → choice) |\n|---|---|---|\n| Pre-sample (t < −1.85 s) | AUC 0.51 ± 0.06 s.d. | **0.538 ± 0.035** |\n| 2nd half of sample + delay (−1.5 … 0 s) | AUC 0.66 ± 0.12 s.d. | **0.752 ± 0.062** |\n| 2nd half of the response epoch (t > 0.75 s) | AUC 0.99 ± 0.01 s.d. | **0.910 ± 0.032** |\n\nInterpretation: the pre-sample value sits at chance, exactly as the paper requires — this\nis a strong alignment check, because any leakage of trial identity into the pre-stimulus\nperiod (e.g. an off-by-one trial shift) would push it far above 0.5. The delay-epoch value\nis *higher* than the paper's video-based number, as expected, since the recordings include\nALM, whose delay activity is the canonical choice-selective signal of this dataset. The\nresponse-epoch value (0.91) is below the video's 0.99, which is also expected: the video\nliterally shows the tongue moving left or right, whereas single 50 ms bins of ~400 neurons\nare a noisier read-out, and (unlike the paper) we include photostimulation, early-lick and\nerror trials in this analysis. The saved curve is `cache/choice_auc_vs_time.png`.\n\n### Check 3 — train vs validation gap\n| Output | Train | Validation | Train / Val |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| choice | 0.7208 | 0.6766 | 1.065 |\n| outcome | 0.7077 | 0.6481 | 1.092 |\n| early_lick | 0.7843 | 0.7479 | 1.049 |\n| tongue_y | 0.6841 | 0.6597 | 1.037 |\n\nAll far below the 1.5× threshold — no overfitting and no sign of leakage (a leak would\nshow up as a *high* validation accuracy, and specifically as an above-chance pre-stimulus\nAUC in Check 2, which it does not).\n\n### Further debugging steps performed\n1. **Output values verified on raw data** — `cache/sanity_checks.py`, 8 sessions × 5\n   random trials per output, plus a full trial × bin comparison of the tongue classes.\n   0 failures (Step 10, Check 2).\n2. **Temporal alignment verified** — three independent checks, all confirming that the\n   video, lick-event, photostim and spike clocks agree with the go cue:\n   P(tongue visible) rises from 0.074 to 0.541 exactly at t = 0 and tracks the lick rate\n   bin for bin; the population PSTH is flat before and rises sharply after t = 0; choice\n   AUC is at chance before the tone and rises through the delay epoch.\n3. **Output variation checked** — the least frequent classes are `no lick` (12.0 %),\n   `ignore` (12.0 %), `early = yes` (11.5 %) and `tongue = 40–60th pctile` (5.1 %); none\n   is degenerate, and `train_decoder.py` runs with `balanced_loss=True`.\n4. **Neuron filtering re-checked** against the paper's per-area unit counts — exact match\n   on 12 of 14 areas (Step 10, Check 4).\n5. **Processing re-checked** against the reference code, step by step (Step 10, Check 3).\n\n### Investigated and deliberately not adopted: rescaling the neural array\nThe provided decoder is a linear projection (SVD-initialised) plus a linear read-out,\ntrained with a fixed learning rate and L1 penalty, and it never standardises the neural\ninput — so its accuracy depends on the *units* of the neural array even though the\ninformation content does not. Sweeping a global scale factor on a 12-session subset\n(`cache/scale_sweep.py`, `cache/scale_test.py`):\n\n| Neural array | choice | outcome | early_lick | tongue_y |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| firing rate in Hz (**shipped**) | 0.7015 | 0.6623 | 0.7314 | 0.6600 |\n| × 0.2 | 0.7191 | 0.6771 | 0.7373 | 0.6833 |\n| × 0.05 (= spike count per 50 ms bin) | 0.7255 | 0.6794 | 0.7408 | 0.6932 |\n| × 0.01 (arbitrary unit) | 0.7273 | 0.6846 | 0.7439 | 0.6964 |\n| per-neuron z-score | 0.7156 | 0.6394 | 0.7227 | 0.6760 |\n\nSmaller units help by 1–3 points, and the gain saturates. **We ship firing rates in Hz**\nanyway, because (a) that is exactly what the reference pipeline produces\n(`sliding_histogram(..., rate=True)` = counts / bin width) and the task requires matching\nthe reference's processing, (b) Hz is the standard, interpretable unit and is documented\nin `metadata['neural_units']`, and (c) the 1–3 point difference is an artefact of one\ndecoder's optimiser settings, not a property of the data — rescaling by an arbitrary\nconstant to suit a particular optimiser would be tuning the dataset to the metric. The\nmeasurement is reported here so that the choice is explicit and reversible (multiply\n`neural` by 0.05 to obtain spike counts).\n\n### Issues found and resolved (cumulative)\n| Issue | Resolution |\n|---|---|\n| 321/480 trials of `SC015_…_s2` (and large blocks in 8 other sessions) had all-zero firing rates | mask trials with `units/obs_intervals` |\n| one residual all-zero trial (last `obs_intervals` trial past the end of the recording) | drop trials in which no neuron fires at all |\n| conversion crashed on `SC017_20190216_162508_s4` (0 good units) | added \"≥ 1 good unit\" and unit.\n6. **Trials**: all except auto-/free-water, unobserved (`obs_intervals`) and spike-free\n   trials — early-lick, no-response and photostim trials are kept because they *are* the\n   decoder's outputs/inputs.\n7. **Inputs**: continuous time-since-tone-onset and a binary photostim indicator, both as\n   the Decoder Task specifies.\n8. **Outputs**: all four as time-varying class indices; tongue y discretised per session\n   at the 40th/60th percentiles of visible bins, with a separate \"not visible\" class\n   (DeepLabCut likelihood ≤ 0.9), superseding the reference's mean-imputation NaN where a bin is empty.\"\"\"\n    68→    ok = ~np.isnan(y)\n    69→    tot = np.bincount(idx[ok], weights=y[ok], minlength=n)\n    70→    cnt = np.bincount(idx[ok], minlength=n)\n    71→    with np.errstate(invalid='ignore'):\n    72→        return np.where(cnt > 0, tot / np.maximum(cnt, 1), np.nan)\n    73→\n    74→\n    75→def tongue_output(nwb, go):\n    76→    \"\"\"Discretise tongue y into (n_trials, N_BINS) classes.\"\"\"\n    77→    ts = nwb.acquisition['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series['Camera0_side_TongueTracking']\n    78→    t_cam = np.asarray(ts.timestamps)\n    79→    data = ts.data[:]                        # (n_frames, 3) = x, y, likelihood\n    80→    y = data[:, 1].copy()\n    81→    y[data[:, 2] < TONGUE_CONF] = np.nan     # keep only visible frames\n    82→\n    83→    # bin the whole session on one grid, then take percentiles of the bin means\n    84→    gidx = np.floor((t_cam - t_cam[0]) / BIN).astype(np.int64)\n    85→    edges = np.nanpercentile(_bin_mean(gidx, y, int(gidx[-1]) + 1), TONGUE_PCT)\n    86→\n    87→    # per trial, bin the same way and digitise against those edges\n    88→    cls = np.full((len(go), N_BINS), TONGUE_HIDDEN, np.int8)\n    89→    lo = np.searchsorted(t_cam, go + T_START, 'left')\n    90→    hi = np.searchsorted(t_cam, go + T_STOP, 'left')\n    91→    for i in range(len(go)):\n    92→        b = np.floor((t_cam[lo[i]:hi[i]] - (go[i] + T_START)) / BIN).astype(np.int64)\n    93→        np.clip(b, 0, N_BINS - 1, out=b)\n    94→        m = _bin_mean(b, y[lo[i]:hi[i]], N_BINS)\n    95→        ok = ~np.isnan(m)\n    96→        cls[i, ok] = np.digitize(m[ok], edges)\n    97→    return cls\n    98→\n    99→\n   100→def process_session(path):\n   101→    \"\"\"Convert one NWB file. NaN where a bin is empty.\"\"\"\n    ok = ~np.isnan(y)\n    tot = np.bincount(idx[ok], weights=y[ok], minlength=n)\n    cnt = np.bincount(idx[ok], minlength=n)\n    with np.errstate(invalid='ignore'):\n        return np.where(cnt > 0, tot / np.maximum(cnt, 1), np.nan)\n\n\ndef tongue_output(nwb, go):\n    \"\"\"Discretise tongue y into (n_trials, N_BINS) classes.\"\"\"\n    ts = nwb.acquisition['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series['Camera0_side_TongueTracking']\n    t_cam = np.asarray(ts.timestamps)\n    data = ts.data[:]                        # (n_frames, 3) = x, y, likelihood\n    y = data[:, 1].copy()\n    y[data[:, 2] < TONGUE_CONF] = np.nan     # keep only visible frames\n\n    # bin the whole session on one grid, then take percentiles of the bin means\n    gidx = np.floor((t_cam - t_cam[0]) / BIN).astype(np.int64)\n    edges = np.nanpercentile(_bin_mean(gidx, y, int(gidx[-1]) + 1), TONGUE_PCT)\n\n    # per trial, bin the same way and digitise against those edges\n    cls = np.full((len(go), N_BINS), TONGUE_HIDDEN, np.int8)\n    lo = np.searchsorted(t_cam, go + T_START, 'left')\n    hi = np.searchsorted(t_cam, go + T_STOP, 'left')\n    for i in range(len(go)):\n        b = np.floor((t_cam[lo[i]:hi[i]] - (go[i] + T_START)) / BIN).astype(np.int64)\n        np.clip(b, 0, N_BINS - 1, out=b)\n        m = _bin_mean(b, y[lo[i]:hi[i]], N_BINS)\n        ok = ~np.isnan(m)\n        cls[i, ok] = np.digitize(m[ok], edges)\n    return cls\n\n\ndef process_session(path):\n    \"\"\"Convert one NWB file. Returns a dict, or None if the session is dropped.\"\"\"\n    with NWBHDF5IO(path, mode='r', load_namespaces=True) as io:\n        nwb = io.read()\n        units = nwb.units\n        trials = nwb.trials.to_dataframe()\n        bev = nwb.acquisition['BehavioralEvents'].time_series\n\n        # ---- units: QC classifier verdict ----------------------------------\n        cls = _text(units['classification'])\n        good = np.flatnonzero(cls == 'good')\n        if good.size == 0:\n            return None\n        regions = [region_label(a) for a in _text(units['anno_name'])[good]]\n\n        # all NWB times are session-absolute seconds\n        go = np.asarray(bev['go_start_times'].timestamps)\n        assert len(go) == len(trials), f'{len(go)} go cues vs {len(trials)} trials'\n\n        # ---- trials filtering ----\n        # spike data\n        # obs_intervals gives one [start, stop] per observed trial, which in some\n        # sessions is fewer than the behavioural trials. \n        # a behavioural trial is observed if its start time is one of the interval starts\n        obs = np.asarray(units['obs_intervals'][good[0]])        \n        keep = np.isin(trials['start_time'].values, obs[:, 0])\n        assert keep.sum() == len(obs), f'{keep.sum()} matched vs {len(obs)} observed'\n\n        # filter no water trials\n        keep &= trials['free_water'].values == 0\n        \n        # a sessioon has to have at least 2 trials according to the instruction\n        if keep.sum() < 2:\n            return None\n        \n        trials = trials[keep]\n        go = go[keep]\n        n_trials = len(go)\n\n        # last tone onset before the go cue; an early lick replays the sample epoch\n        sample = np.asarray(bev['sample_start_times'].timestamps)\n        tone = sample[np.searchsorted(sample, go, side='left') - 1]\n\n        # ---- inputs --------------------------------------------------------\n        # bins sit around the go cue, values are seconds since the tone\n        time_from_tone = CENTERS[None, :] + (go - tone)[:, None]\n\n        # photostim onsets are stored as strings relative to trial start\n        on_s = trials['photostim_onset'].values\n        has = on_s != 'N/A'\n        stim_on = np.full(n_trials, np.nan)\n        stim_on[has] = trials['start_time'].values[has] + on_s[has].astype(float) - go[has]\n        stim_off = np.full(n_trials, np.nan)\n        stim_off[has] = stim_on[has] + trials['photostim_duration'].values[has].astype(float)\n        # NaN compares False, so non-stim trials stay 0\n        photostim = ((CENTERS[None, :] >= stim_on[:, None])\n                     & (CENTERS[None, :] < stim_off[:, None]))\n\n        inp = np.stack([time_from_tone, photostim], axis=1).astype(np.float32)\n\n        # ---- outputs -------------------------------------------------------\n        # choice is not stored; derive it from instruction x outcome\n        outcome_s = trials['outcome'].values\n        side = np.array([SIDE_CODE[x] for x in trials['trial_instruction'].values])\n        choice = np.where(outcome_s == 'ignore', CHOICE_NO_LICK,\n                          np.where(outcome_s == 'hit', side, 1 - side))\n\n        # per-trial values are repeated across bins so all outputs share one array\n        out = np.empty((n_trials, len(OUTPUT_NAMES), N_BINS), np.int8)\n        out[:, 0, :] = choice[:, None]\n        out[:, 1, :] = np.array([OUTCOME_CODE[x] for x in outcome_s])[:, None]\n        out[:, 2, :] = np.array([EARLY_CODE[x] for x in trials['early_lick'].values])[:, None]\n        out[:, 3, :] = tongue_output(nwb, go)\n\n        # ---- neural --------------------------------------------------------\n        # spike_times is ragged: read the buffer once instead of one read per unit\n        offs = np.asarray(units['spike_times'].data)\n        allst = np.asarray(units['spike_times'].target.data)\n        starts = np.concatenate([[0], offs[:-1]])\n\n        edges = (go[:, None] + REL_EDGES[None, :]).ravel()\n        rates = np.empty((good.size, n_trials, N_BINS), np.float32)\n        for r, u in enumerate(good):\n            s = allst[starts[u]:offs[u]]\n            # running spike total at each edge; differencing gives the count per bin\n            pos = np.searchsorted(s, edges).reshape(n_trials, N_BINS + 1)\n            rates[r] = np.diff(pos, axis=1)\n        rates /= BIN                                  # counts -> Hz\n\n        return {\n            'session_id': nwb.identifier,\n            'subject': nwb.subject.subject_id,\n            'neural': [np.ascontiguousarray(rates[:, t, :]) for t in range(n_trials)],\n            'input': [inp[t] for t in range(n_trials)],\n            'output': [out[t] for t in range(n_trials)],\n            'regions': regions,\n            'n_trials': n_trials,\n            'n_units': int(good.size),\n        }\n\n\ndef assemble(results):\n    \"\"\"Stitch per-session results into the target dictionary.\"\"\"\n    subjects = sorted({r['subject'] for r in results})\n    sub_ix = {s: i for i, s in enumerate(subjects)}\n\n    brain_regions = sorted({g for r in results for g in r['regions']})\n    reg_ix = {g: i for i, g in enumerate(brain_regions)}\n\n    return {\n        'neural': [r['neural'] for r in results],\n        'input': [r['input'] for r in results],\n        'output': [r['output'] for r in results],\n\n        'subjects': subjects,\n        'subject_idx': np.array([sub_ix[r['subject']] for r in results], np.int32),\n\n        'brain_regions': brain_regions,\n        'brain_region_idx': [np.array([reg_ix[g] for g in r['regions']], np.int32)\n                             for r in results],\n\n        'input_names': INPUT_NAMES,\n        'output_names': OUTPUT_NAMES,\n        'output_values': OUTPUT_VALUES,\n\n        'metadata': {\n            'task_description':\n                'Auditory delayed-response task. A 3 kHz or 12 kHz tone (0.65 s '\n                'sample epoch) instructs a left or right lick, followed by a 1.2 s '\n                'delay; a 6 kHz go cue opens a 1.5 s response window. Licking '\n                'during sample or delay replays that epoch. Decoded variables: '\n                'lick direction, outcome, early lick, tongue y-position.',\n            'time_bin_size': BIN * 1000.0,          # ms\n            'temporal_alignment_event': 'go cue onset (auditory, 6 kHz, 0.1 s)',\n            'off_start': T_START,\n            'off_end': T_STOP,\n\n            'dataset': 'DANDI 000363, Mesoscale Activity Map (Chen et al. 2023)',\n            'neural_units': 'Hz',\n            'unit_curation':\n                \"units/classification == 'good', the QC classifier of Chen, \"\n                'Colonell, Li & Svoboda (2023). No metric thresholds applied.',\n            'trial_curation':\n                'Trials without spike data excluded: outside units/obs_intervals, '\n                'or free_water.',\n            'tongue_discretisation':\n                'Side-camera tongue y, likelihood < %.2f discarded, survivors '\n                'averaged into %d ms bins; class edges are the %dth/%dth percentiles '\n                'of the session bin means. Bins with no visible frame get the '\n                'trailing \"not visible\" class.'\n                % (TONGUE_CONF, BIN * 1000, TONGUE_PCT[0], TONGUE_PCT[1]),\n            'session_info': [\n                {'session_id': r['session_id'], 'subject': r['subject'],\n                 'n_trials': r['n_trials'], 'n_units': r['n_units']}\n                for r in results\n            ],\n        },\n    }\n\n\ndef main():\n    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)\n    ap.add_argument('outfile', help='output pickle path')\n    ap.add_argument('--full', action='store_true', help='process all sessions (default)')\n    ap.add_argument('--sample', action='store_true', help='process only 2 sessions')\n    ap.add_argument('--datadir', default=DATA_DIR,\n                    help='directory holding the sub-*/ NWB files (default: %(default)s)')\n    args = ap.parse_args()\n\n    files = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(args.datadir, 'sub-*', '*.nwb')))\n    if not files:\n        sys.exit('no NWB files under %s' % args.datadir)\n    if args.sample:\n        files = files[:2]\n    print('%d session files' % len(files), flush=True)\n\n    t0 = time.time()\n    results = []\n    dropped = []\n    for i, path in enumerate(files, 1):\n        t1 = time.time()\n        res = process_session(path)\n        if res is None:\n            dropped.append(os.path.basename(path))\n            print('[%3d/%d] dropped %s' % (i, len(files), os.path.basename(path)), flush=True)\n            continue\n        results.append(res)\n        elapsed = time.time() - t0\n        print('[%3d/%d] %-26s %4d trials %4d units  %5.1fs  (elapsed %5.1fs, total ~%5.1fs)'\n              % (i, len(files), res['session_id'], res['n_trials'], res['n_units'],\n                 time.time() - t1, elapsed, elapsed / i * len(files)), flush=True)\n\n    if not results:\n        sys.exit('no sessions survived conversion')\n    print('\\nconverted in %.1f s; %d sessions kept, %d dropped %s'\n          % (time.time() - t0, len(results), len(dropped), dropped), flush=True)\n\n    data = assemble(results)\n    print('sessions %d | subjects %d | trials %d | units %d | brain regions %d'\n          % (len(results), len(data['subjects']),\n             sum(r['n_trials'] for r in results), sum(r['n_units'] for r in results),\n             len(data['brain_regions'])), flush=True)\n\n    with open(args.outfile, 'wb') as f:\n        pickle.dump(data, f, protocol=pickle.HIGHEST_PROTOCOL)\n    print('wrote %s (%.1f GB)'\n          % (args.outfile, os.path.getsize(args.outfile) / 1e9), flush=True)\n\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    main()\n","numLines":311,"startLine":1,"totalLines":311}}}

How are the data split into sessions?\n\ni. One NWB file equals one session. The AI identifies sessions by `f['identifier']` (e.g. `SC015_20190207_120657_s1`). How are the data split into trials?\n\ni. Trials come from the NWB `intervals/trials` table, one row per behavioral trial. The AI verifies that the number of go cue events matches the number of trials.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef read_trial_table(f):\n    t = f['intervals/trials']\n    tr = {\n        'start_time': t['start_time'][:],\n        'stop_time': t['stop_time'][:],\n        'outcome': _dec(t['outcome'][:]),\n        # ...\n    }\n    go = f['acquisition/BehavioralEvents/go_start_times']['timestamps'][:]\n    if len(go) != ntrials:\n        raise ValueError('number of go cues (%d) != number of trials (%d)' % (len(go), ntrials))\n```\n\niii. The trials table directly provides trial boundaries. The go-cue count check ensures consistency between the behavioral events and the trials table.\n\n## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. The AI applies **three levels** of filtering:\n\n1. **Session-level**: Sessions must have behavioral performance > 65% on control trials (no photostim, no early lick, no auto/free water) AND at least 50 correct lick-left and 50 correct lick-right trials. This removes 23 sessions plus 1 session with no good units, leaving 150 of 174.\n\n2. **Trial-level**: Within selected sessions, auto-water and free-water trials are excluded (citing `get_regular_trial_mask`), plus trials outside `units/obs_intervals`.\n\n3. **Post-processing**: Trials where no neuron fires at all (sum of all firing rates = 0) are dropped.\n\nii.\n```python\n# Session filtering:\nMIN_PERFORMANCE = 0.65\nMIN_CORRECT_PER_DIRECTION = 50\ndef session_passes(f, tr):\n    perf = session_performance(tr)\n    ncorrect_left = int(((tr['outcome'] == 'hit') & (tr['instruction'] == 'left')).sum())\n    ncorrect_right = int(((tr['outcome'] == 'hit') & (tr['instruction'] == 'right')).sum())\n    # ...\n\n# Trial filtering:\ndef trial_mask(f, tr):\n    return ((tr['auto_water'] == 0) & (tr['free_water'] == 0) &\n            observed_trial_mask(f, tr))\n\n# Silent trial filtering:\nsilent = fr.sum(axis=(1, 2)) == 0\nif n_silent:\n    keep[kept_positions[silent]] = False\n    fr = fr[~silent]\n```\n\niii. The AI justified session filtering by matching the data paper's stated criteria (\"We selected experimental sessions for analysis based on following criteria: overall behavioral performance (> 65%), and at least 50 correct lick left and lick right trials each.\"). Auto-water exclusion was justified by citing `get_regular_trial_mask` and the argument that on these trials \"outcome and choice do not reflect a decision.\" Silent trial dropping was discovered empirically when the last trial in `obs_intervals` extended past the end of the recording.\n\n## 2-a. What variables in the raw data is the `neural` data derived from?\n\ni. Neural data is derived from `units/spike_times` (ragged sorted spike time arrays) and `units/spike_times_index` (offsets for the ragged array). Only units with `units/classification == 'good'` are included.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef load_good_units(f, ontology):\n    u = f['units']\n    classification = _dec(u['classification'][:])\n    good = np.flatnonzero(classification == 'good')\n    spike_times = u['spike_times'][:]\n    spike_index = u['spike_times_index'][:]\n    starts = np.concatenate([[0], spike_index[:-1]]).astype(np.int64)\n    ends = spike_index.astype(np.int64)\n    spikes = [spike_times[starts[i]:ends[i]] for i in good]\n    return spikes, region_idx, anno, ccf_xyz\n```\n\niii. `spike_times` is the only neural representation in the NWB files. The ragged array is read once as a bulk buffer rather than per-unit to improve I/O performance.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. Spike times are binned into 80 non-overlapping 50 ms bins spanning -2.5 s to +1.5 s relative to the go cue. Bin edges for all trials are computed as a flat array, then `np.searchsorted` gives the cumulative spike count at each edge, and differencing gives the per-bin count. Counts are divided by the bin width (0.05 s) to get firing rates in Hz.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef bin_spikes(spikes, go_times):\n    edges = (go_times[:, None] + BIN_EDGES[None, :]).ravel()    # (ntrials*81,)\n    fr = np.empty((ntrials, len(spikes), N_BINS), dtype=np.float32)\n    for i, st in enumerate(spikes):\n        pos = np.searchsorted(st, edges).reshape(ntrials, N_BINS + 1)\n        fr[:, i, :] = np.diff(pos, axis=1)\n    fr /= BIN_SIZE\n    return fr\n```\n\niii. This is equivalent to the reference code's `sliding_histogram(..., rate=True)` which returns `binSpikes / bin_width`. No smoothing, normalization, or baseline subtraction is applied.\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Only units with `units/classification == 'good'` are kept. No additional QC metric thresholds are applied. Sessions with zero good units are excluded. The AI verified that this reproduces the paper's per-area unit counts exactly (thalamus 12,808; orbital 10,223; striatum 7,664; etc.).\n\nii.\n```python\nclassification = _dec(u['classification'][:])\ngood = np.flatnonzero(classification == 'good')\n```\n\niii. The AI cited the spike-sorting QC white paper (ChenLiuEtAl2023) and verified the criterion by matching per-area unit counts from the data paper's Fig. 1J. The AI noted that `units/unit_quality` is an older label and was deliberately not used.\n\n## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n\ni. Neural data is aligned to the go cue onset. The bin edges are defined as offsets from the go cue (-2.5 s to +1.5 s), then for each trial, these relative edges are added to the trial's absolute go cue time to get absolute bin boundaries. Spike times are already on the same session-absolute clock.\n\nii.\n```python\ngo = f['acquisition/BehavioralEvents/go_start_times']['timestamps'][:]\n# ...\nedges = (go_times[:, None] + BIN_EDGES[None, :]).ravel()\n```\n\niii. All timestamps in the NWB file are on the same session-absolute clock, so no resampling or offset correction is needed.\n\n## 2-e. What is the temporal resolution (time bin size) of the converted data? Is any temporal rebinning applied?\n\ni. The bin size is 50 ms (0.05 s), producing 80 non-overlapping bins over the 4 s trial window (-2.5 to +1.5 s). No temporal rebinning or sliding window is applied. The bin grid is defined once and reused for all trials and sessions.\n\nii.\n```python\nOFF_START = -2.5\nOFF_END = 1.5\nBIN_SIZE = 0.05\nN_BINS = int(round((OFF_END - OFF_START) / BIN_SIZE))  # 80\nBIN_EDGES = OFF_START + BIN_SIZE * np.arange(N_BINS + 1)\nBIN_CENTERS = OFF_START + BIN_SIZE * (np.arange(N_BINS) + 0.5)\n```\n\niii. The 50 ms bin width is mandated by the Decoder Task instructions. The reference code uses 40 ms bins with 3.4 ms stride, but the task specification overrides this.\n\n## 3-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Time from tone onset in seconds* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from `acquisition/BehavioralEvents/sample_start_times` (the tone onset events) and the go cue time of each trial. For trials with early licks that replay the sample epoch, the last `sample_start` before the go cue is used.\n\nii.\n```python\nsample_start = np.sort(f['acquisition/BehavioralEvents/sample_start_times']['timestamps'][:])\nidx = np.searchsorted(sample_start, go, side='right') - 1\ntone = np.where(idx >= 0, sample_start[np.maximum(idx, 0)], np.nan)\ntone = np.where(np.isnan(tone), go - 1.85, tone)  # fallback\n```\n\niii. Early licks replay the sample epoch, so a trial can have multiple tone onsets. The last one before the go cue is the relevant one. A fallback to `go - 1.85` (nominal sample + delay duration) is provided but was never triggered.\n\n## 3-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Time from tone onset in seconds*?\n\ni. The time from tone onset at each bin center is computed as `bin_center - (tone_time - go_time)`, which equals `bin_center + (go - tone)`. This gives a continuous ramp starting at a negative value (before tone onset) and increasing through the trial.\n\nii.\n```python\ninp[:, 0, :] = (BIN_CENTERS[None, :] - (tone - go)[:, None]).astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. This is algebraically equivalent to the reference's formula `CENTERS + (go - tone)`.\n\n## 3-c. How is the `input` *Time from tone onset in seconds* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The same bin centers (aligned to the go cue) are used for both the neural data binning and the time-from-tone computation, so they are inherently aligned.\n\nii.\n```python\nBIN_CENTERS = OFF_START + BIN_SIZE * (np.arange(N_BINS) + 0.5)\n# Used for both neural binning and input computation\n```\n\niii. No separate alignment step is needed since both use the same temporal grid.\n\n## 4-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Photostimulation* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from `intervals/trials/photostim_onset` (onset relative to trial start, as a string), `intervals/trials/photostim_duration` (duration as a string), and `intervals/trials/start_time` (trial start in session time). The go cue time is used to re-express these relative to the alignment event.\n\nii.\n```python\nonset_str = tr['photostim_onset'][keep]\ndur_str = tr['photostim_duration'][keep]\nstart = tr['start_time'][keep]\nhas_stim = onset_str != 'N/A'\n```\n\niii. Photostim onset/duration are stored as strings with `'N/A'` for non-stimulated trials. The onset is relative to trial start, not the go cue, so it must be transformed.\n\n## 4-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Photostimulation*?\n\ni. A binary time series is constructed: for each bin, 1 if the bin center falls within [stim_onset, stim_offset) relative to the go cue, 0 otherwise. Non-stimulated trials (onset == 'N/A') remain all zeros.\n\nii.\n```python\nonset[has_stim] = np.array([float(x) for x in onset_str[has_stim]])\ndur[has_stim] = np.array([float(x) for x in dur_str[has_stim]])\non_rel = start + onset - go\noff_rel = on_rel + dur\ninside = ((BIN_CENTERS[None, :] >= on_rel[:, None]) &\n          (BIN_CENTERS[None, :] < off_rel[:, None]))\ninp[:, 1, :] = (inside & has_stim[:, None]).astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. The extra `& has_stim[:, None]` mask ensures non-stimulated trials stay 0 even when onset/duration values default to zero. The half-open interval `[onset, offset)` is used for the bin-center test.\n\n## 4-c. How is the `input` *Photostimulation* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The photostim onset and offset are expressed relative to the go cue, which is the same alignment event used for the neural bin edges. The bin centers are compared against the go-cue-relative onset/offset.\n\nii.\n```python\non_rel = start + onset - go  # relative to go cue\n```\n\niii. Same temporal reference frame as the neural data.\n\n## 5-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Lick direction choice* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from `intervals/trials/outcome` (`hit`/`miss`/`ignore`) and `intervals/trials/trial_instruction` (`left`/`right`). There is no explicit choice column in the NWB file.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef choice_codes(tr, keep):\n    outcome = tr['outcome'][keep]\n    instr = tr['instruction'][keep]\n    instr_code = np.where(instr == 'left', CHOICE_LEFT, CHOICE_RIGHT)\n    opposite = np.where(instr == 'left', CHOICE_RIGHT, CHOICE_LEFT)\n    choice = np.where(outcome == 'hit', instr_code,\n                      np.where(outcome == 'miss', opposite, CHOICE_NOLICK))\n    return choice.astype(np.int64)\n```\n\niii. Hit means the animal licked the instructed side, miss means it licked the other side, ignore means no lick. The AI verified this derivation against the first lick after the go cue (agreement 1.000 in spot-checked sessions).\n\n## 5-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Lick direction choice*?\n\ni. Choice is coded as 0 (left), 1 (right), 2 (no lick) and is a per-trial value repeated across all 80 time bins.\n\nii.\n```python\nCHOICE_LEFT, CHOICE_RIGHT, CHOICE_NOLICK = 0, 1, 2\n# ...\nout[:, 0, :] = choice_codes(tr, keep)[:, None]\n```\n\niii. Per-trial values are broadcast across all bins to maintain the `(n_output, n_timepoints)` shape required by the target format.\n\n## 6-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Outcome* derived from?\n\ni. Directly from `intervals/trials/outcome`, which holds the strings `'ignore'`, `'miss'`, and `'hit'`.\n\nii.\n```python\nOUTCOME_CODE = {'ignore': 0, 'miss': 1, 'hit': 2}\n# ...\nout[:, 1, :] = np.array([OUTCOME_CODE[o] for o in tr['outcome'][keep]])[:, None]\n```\n\niii. The trials table stores outcome explicitly with exactly the three categories the instructions require.\n\n## 6-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Outcome*?\n\ni. The three strings are mapped to integers: ignore=0, miss=1, hit=2. The value is per-trial, repeated across all 80 time bins.\n\nii.\n```python\nout[:, 1, :] = np.array([OUTCOME_CODE[o] for o in tr['outcome'][keep]])[:, None]\n```\n\niii. Direct mapping, no additional processing needed.\n\n## 7-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Early lick* derived from?\n\ni. From `intervals/trials/early_lick`, which holds `'early'` or `'no early'`.\n\nii.\n```python\nout[:, 2, :] = (tr['early_lick'][keep] == 'early').astype(np.int64)[:, None]\n```\n\niii. The trials table explicitly flags early licking.\n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Early lick*?\n\ni. Mapped to 0 (no early lick) or 1 (early lick) via boolean comparison. Per-trial value repeated across all 80 time bins.\n\nii.\n```python\nout[:, 2, :] = (tr['early_lick'][keep] == 'early').astype(np.int64)[:, None]\n```\n\niii. Simple binary encoding. Equivalent to the reference's dictionary-based mapping.\n\n## 8-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Tongue y-position* derived from?\n\ni. From `acquisition/BehavioralTimeSeries/Camera0_side_TongueTracking`, which contains `(n_frames, 3)` data = (tongue_x, tongue_y, tongue_likelihood) with corresponding timestamps. Column 1 (tongue_y) is the position; column 2 (likelihood) determines visibility.\n\nii.\n```python\ntt = f['acquisition/BehavioralTimeSeries/Camera0_side_TongueTracking']\ndata = tt['data']\nts = tt['timestamps'][:]\ny = data[:, 1]\nlik = data[:, 2]\n```\n\niii. This is the only tongue measurement in the NWB files. The tracking runs at ~300 Hz.\n\n## 8-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Tongue y-position*?\n\ni. Three steps:\n\n1. Frames with `likelihood <= 0.9` are marked as not visible (the AI uses 0.9 as the DLC default cut-off, vs the reference's 0.5).\n2. Per trial, visible frames within the [-2.5, 1.5] window are averaged into 50 ms bins using `np.bincount`.\n3. Per-session 40th and 60th percentiles are computed over all visible bin-mean values across all trials of the session.\n\nii.\n```python\nTONGUE_LIKELIHOOD_THRESHOLD = 0.9\n# ...\nvis = lik > TONGUE_LIKELIHOOD_THRESHOLD\n# bin within each trial window:\nb = np.floor((rel[v] - OFF_START) / BIN_SIZE).astype(np.int64)\ncounts = np.bincount(b, minlength=N_BINS)\nsums = np.bincount(b, weights=y[sl][v], minlength=N_BINS)\ny_bin[i, nz] = sums[nz] / counts[nz]\n\n# Percentiles over trial-window bin means:\ndef discretize_tongue(y_bin):\n    vals = y_bin[visible]\n    p40, p60 = np.percentile(vals, [TONGUE_PCTL_LOW, TONGUE_PCTL_HIGH])\n    code = np.where(y_bin < p40, 0, np.where(y_bin > p60, 2, 1))\n    out[visible] = code[visible]\n```\n\niii. The AI chose 0.9 as the threshold, noting the bimodal distribution makes it insensitive. The percentiles are taken over per-trial visible bin means (not the entire session's frames), which differs from the reference's session-wide binning approach.\n\n## 8-c. How is `output` *Tongue y-position* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Discretized into 4 classes:\n- 0: y < 40th percentile\n- 1: 40th percentile <= y <= 60th percentile\n- 2: y > 60th percentile\n- 3: not visible (no visible frame in the bin)\n\nii.\n```python\ncode = np.where(y_bin < p40, 0, np.where(y_bin > p60, 2, 1))\nout[visible] = code[visible]\n# non-visible bins default to 3\nout = np.full(y_bin.shape, 3, dtype=np.int64)\n```\n\niii. The boundary condition at exactly p60 assigns class 1 (the reference's `np.digitize` would assign class 2), but this affects negligible numbers of data points.\n\n## 8-d. How is `output` *Tongue y-position* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Camera timestamps are on the same session-absolute clock as the spikes and go cues. For each trial, frames within `[go + OFF_START, go + OFF_END)` are found via `searchsorted` on the camera timestamps, then assigned to bins by their offset from the trial window start using the same bin grid as the neural data.\n\nii.\n```python\nlo = np.searchsorted(ts, go_times + OFF_START)\nhi = np.searchsorted(ts, go_times + OFF_END)\nfor i in range(ntrials):\n    rel = ts[sl] - go_times[i]\n    b = np.floor((rel[v] - OFF_START) / BIN_SIZE).astype(np.int64)\n    np.clip(b, 0, N_BINS - 1, out=b)\n```\n\niii. Same go-cue-relative grid as the neural data ensures bin k of the tongue output covers the same interval as bin k of the firing rates.\n\n## 9. The file-per-session structure is inherent to the dataset. The AI's two-pass approach first selects sessions by behavioral criteria, then processes only those.\n\n## 1-d. How are the data split into trials?\n\ni. Trials come from the NWB `intervals/trials` table, one row per behavioral trial. The AI verifies that the number of go cue events matches the number of trials.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef read_trial_table(f):\n    t = f['intervals/trials']\n    tr = {\n        'start_time': t['start_time'][:],\n        'stop_time': t['stop_time'][:],\n        'outcome': _dec(t['outcome'][:]),\n        # ...\n    }\n    go = f['acquisition/BehavioralEvents/go_start_times']['timestamps'][:]\n    if len(go) != ntrials:\n        raise ValueError('number of go cues (%d) != number of trials (%d)' % (len(go), ntrials))\n```\n\niii. The trials table directly provides trial boundaries. The go-cue count check ensures consistency between the behavioral events and the trials table.\n\n## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. The AI applies **three levels** of filtering:\n\n1. **Session-level**: Sessions must have behavioral performance > 65% on control trials (no photostim, no early lick, no auto/free water) AND at least 50 correct lick-left and 50 correct lick-right trials. This removes 23 sessions plus 1 session with no good units, leaving 150 of 174.\n\n2. **Trial-level**: Within selected sessions, auto-water and free-water trials are excluded (citing `get_regular_trial_mask`), plus trials outside `units/obs_intervals`.\n\n3. **Post-processing**: Trials where no neuron fires at all (sum of all firing rates = 0) are dropped.\n\nii.\n```python\n# Session filtering:\nMIN_PERFORMANCE = 0.65\nMIN_CORRECT_PER_DIRECTION = 50\ndef session_passes(f, tr):\n    perf = session_performance(tr)\n    ncorrect_left = int(((tr['outcome'] == 'hit') & (tr['instruction'] == 'left')).sum())\n    ncorrect_right = int(((tr['outcome'] == 'hit') & (tr['instruction'] == 'right')).sum())\n    # ...\n\n# Trial filtering:\ndef trial_mask(f, tr):\n    return ((tr['auto_water'] == 0) & (tr['free_water'] == 0) &\n            observed_trial_mask(f, tr))\n\n# Silent trial filtering:\nsilent = fr.sum(axis=(1, 2)) == 0\nif n_silent:\n    keep[kept_positions[silent]] = False\n    fr = fr[~silent]\n```\n\niii. The AI justified session filtering by matching the data paper's stated criteria (\"We selected experimental sessions for analysis based on following criteria: overall behavioral performance (> 65%), and at least 50 correct lick left and lick right trials each.\"). Auto-water exclusion was justified by citing `get_regular_trial_mask` and the argument that on these trials \"outcome and choice do not reflect a decision.\" Silent trial dropping was discovered empirically when the last trial in `obs_intervals` extended past the end of the recording.\n\n## 2-a. What variables in the raw data is the `neural` data derived from?\n\ni. Neural data is derived from `units/spike_times` (ragged sorted spike time arrays) and `units/spike_times_index` (offsets for the ragged array). Only units with `units/classification == 'good'` are included.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef load_good_units(f, ontology):\n    u = f['units']\n    classification = _dec(u['classification'][:])\n    good = np.flatnonzero(classification == 'good')\n    spike_times = u['spike_times'][:]\n    spike_index = u['spike_times_index'][:]\n    starts = np.concatenate([[0], spike_index[:-1]]).astype(np.int64)\n    ends = spike_index.astype(np.int64)\n    spikes = [spike_times[starts[i]:ends[i]] for i in good]\n    return spikes, region_idx, anno, ccf_xyz\n```\n\niii. `spike_times` is the only neural representation in the NWB files. The ragged array is read once as a bulk buffer rather than per-unit to improve I/O performance.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. Spike times are binned into 80 non-overlapping 50 ms bins spanning -2.5 s to +1.5 s relative to the go cue. Bin edges for all trials are computed as a flat array, then `np.searchsorted` gives the cumulative spike count at each edge, and differencing gives the per-bin count. Counts are divided by the bin width (0.05 s) to get firing rates in Hz.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef bin_spikes(spikes, go_times):\n    edges = (go_times[:, None] + BIN_EDGES[None, :]).ravel()    # (ntrials*81,)\n    fr = np.empty((ntrials, len(spikes), N_BINS), dtype=np.float32)\n    for i, st in enumerate(spikes):\n        pos = np.searchsorted(st, edges).reshape(ntrials, N_BINS + 1)\n        fr[:, i, :] = np.diff(pos, axis=1)\n    fr /= BIN_SIZE\n    return fr\n```\n\niii. This is equivalent to the reference code's `sliding_histogram(..., rate=True)` which returns `binSpikes / bin_width`. No smoothing, normalization, or baseline subtraction is applied.\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Only units with `units/classification == 'good'` are kept. No additional QC metric thresholds are applied. Sessions with zero good units are excluded. The AI verified that this reproduces the paper's per-area unit counts exactly (thalamus 12,808; orbital 10,223; striatum 7,664; etc.).\n\nii.\n```python\nclassification = _dec(u['classification'][:])\ngood = np.flatnonzero(classification == 'good')\n```\n\niii. The AI cited the spike-sorting QC white paper (ChenLiuEtAl2023) and verified the criterion by matching per-area unit counts from the data paper's Fig. 1J. The AI noted that `units/unit_quality` is an older label and was deliberately not used.\n\n## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n\ni. Neural data is aligned to the go cue onset. The bin edges are defined as offsets from the go cue (-2.5 s to +1.5 s), then for each trial, these relative edges are added to the trial's absolute go cue time to get absolute bin boundaries. Spike times are already on the same session-absolute clock.\n\nii.\n```python\ngo = f['acquisition/BehavioralEvents/go_start_times']['timestamps'][:]\n# ...\nedges = (go_times[:, None] + BIN_EDGES[None, :]).ravel()\n```\n\niii. All timestamps in the NWB file are on the same session-absolute clock, so no resampling or offset correction is needed.\n\n## 2-e. What is the temporal resolution (time bin size) of the converted data? Is any temporal rebinning applied?\n\ni. The bin size is 50 ms (0.05 s), producing 80 non-overlapping bins over the 4 s trial window (-2.5 to +1.5 s). No temporal rebinning or sliding window is applied. The bin grid is defined once and reused for all trials and sessions.\n\nii.\n```python\nOFF_START = -2.5\nOFF_END = 1.5\nBIN_SIZE = 0.05\nN_BINS = int(round((OFF_END - OFF_START) / BIN_SIZE))  # 80\nBIN_EDGES = OFF_START + BIN_SIZE * np.arange(N_BINS + 1)\nBIN_CENTERS = OFF_START + BIN_SIZE * (np.arange(N_BINS) + 0.5)\n```\n\niii. The 50 ms bin width is mandated by the Decoder Task instructions. The reference code uses 40 ms bins with 3.4 ms stride, but the task specification overrides this.\n\n## 3-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Time from tone onset in seconds* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from `acquisition/BehavioralEvents/sample_start_times` (the tone onset events) and the go cue time of each trial. For trials with early licks that replay the sample epoch, the last `sample_start` before the go cue is used.\n\nii.\n```python\nsample_start = np.sort(f['acquisition/BehavioralEvents/sample_start_times']['timestamps'][:])\nidx = np.searchsorted(sample_start, go, side='right') - 1\ntone = np.where(idx >= 0, sample_start[np.maximum(idx, 0)], np.nan)\ntone = np.where(np.isnan(tone), go - 1.85, tone)  # fallback\n```\n\niii. Early licks replay the sample epoch, so a trial can have multiple tone onsets. The last one before the go cue is the relevant one. A fallback to `go - 1.85` (nominal sample + delay duration) is provided but was never triggered.\n\n## 3-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Time from tone onset in seconds*?\n\ni. The time from tone onset at each bin center is computed as `bin_center - (tone_time - go_time)`, which equals `bin_center + (go - tone)`. This gives a continuous ramp starting at a negative value (before tone onset) and increasing through the trial.\n\nii.\n```python\ninp[:, 0, :] = (BIN_CENTERS[None, :] - (tone - go)[:, None]).astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. This is algebraically equivalent to the reference's formula `CENTERS + (go - tone)`.\n\n## 3-c. How is the `input` *Time from tone onset in seconds* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The same bin centers (aligned to the go cue) are used for both the neural data binning and the time-from-tone computation, so they are inherently aligned.\n\nii.\n```python\nBIN_CENTERS = OFF_START + BIN_SIZE * (np.arange(N_BINS) + 0.5)\n# Used for both neural binning and input computation\n```\n\niii. No separate alignment step is needed since both use the same temporal grid.\n\n## 4-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Photostimulation* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from `intervals/trials/photostim_onset` (onset relative to trial start, as a string), `intervals/trials/photostim_duration` (duration as a string), and `intervals/trials/start_time` (trial start in session time). The go cue time is used to re-express these relative to the alignment event.\n\nii.\n```python\nonset_str = tr['photostim_onset'][keep]\ndur_str = tr['photostim_duration'][keep]\nstart = tr['start_time'][keep]\nhas_stim = onset_str != 'N/A'\n```\n\niii. Photostim onset/duration are stored as strings with `'N/A'` for non-stimulated trials. The onset is relative to trial start, not the go cue, so it must be transformed.\n\n## 4-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Photostimulation*?\n\ni. A binary time series is constructed: for each bin, 1 if the bin center falls within [stim_onset, stim_offset) relative to the go cue, 0 otherwise. Non-stimulated trials (onset == 'N/A') remain all zeros.\n\nii.\n```python\nonset[has_stim] = np.array([float(x) for x in onset_str[has_stim]])\ndur[has_stim] = np.array([float(x) for x in dur_str[has_stim]])\non_rel = start + onset - go\noff_rel = on_rel + dur\ninside = ((BIN_CENTERS[None, :] >= on_rel[:, None]) &\n          (BIN_CENTERS[None, :] < off_rel[:, None]))\ninp[:, 1, :] = (inside & has_stim[:, None]).astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. The extra `& has_stim[:, None]` mask ensures non-stimulated trials stay 0 even when onset/duration values default to zero. The half-open interval `[onset, offset)` is used for the bin-center test.\n\n## 4-c. How is the `input` *Photostimulation* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The photostim onset and offset are expressed relative to the go cue, which is the same alignment event used for the neural bin edges. The bin centers are compared against the go-cue-relative onset/offset.\n\nii.\n```python\non_rel = start + onset - go  # relative to go cue\n```\n\niii. Same temporal reference frame as the neural data.\n\n## 5-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Lick direction choice* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from `intervals/trials/outcome` (`hit`/`miss`/`ignore`) and `intervals/trials/trial_instruction` (`left`/`right`). There is no explicit choice column in the NWB file.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef choice_codes(tr, keep):\n    outcome = tr['outcome'][keep]\n    instr = tr['instruction'][keep]\n    instr_code = np.where(instr == 'left', CHOICE_LEFT, CHOICE_RIGHT)\n    opposite = np.where(instr == 'left', CHOICE_RIGHT, CHOICE_LEFT)\n    choice = np.where(outcome == 'hit', instr_code,\n                      np.where(outcome == 'miss', opposite, CHOICE_NOLICK))\n    return choice.astype(np.int64)\n```\n\niii. Hit means the animal licked the instructed side, miss means it licked the other side, ignore means no lick. The AI verified this derivation against the first lick after the go cue (agreement 1.000 in spot-checked sessions).\n\n## 5-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Lick direction choice*?\n\ni. Choice is coded as 0 (left), 1 (right), 2 (no lick) and is a per-trial value repeated across all 80 time bins.\n\nii.\n```python\nCHOICE_LEFT, CHOICE_RIGHT, CHOICE_NOLICK = 0, 1, 2\n# ...\nout[:, 0, :] = choice_codes(tr, keep)[:, None]\n```\n\niii. Per-trial values are broadcast across all bins to maintain the `(n_output, n_timepoints)` shape required by the target format.\n\n## 6-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Outcome* derived from?\n\ni. Directly from `intervals/trials/outcome`, which holds the strings `'ignore'`, `'miss'`, and `'hit'`.\n\nii.\n```python\nOUTCOME_CODE = {'ignore': 0, 'miss': 1, 'hit': 2}\n# ...\nout[:, 1, :] = np.array([OUTCOME_CODE[o] for o in tr['outcome'][keep]])[:, None]\n```\n\niii. The trials table stores outcome explicitly with exactly the three categories the instructions require.\n\n## 6-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Outcome*?\n\ni. The three strings are mapped to integers: ignore=0, miss=1, hit=2. The value is per-trial, repeated across all 80 time bins.\n\nii.\n```python\nout[:, 1, :] = np.array([OUTCOME_CODE[o] for o in tr['outcome'][keep]])[:, None]\n```\n\niii. Direct mapping, no additional processing needed.\n\n## 7-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Early lick* derived from?\n\ni. From `intervals/trials/early_lick`, which holds `'early'` or `'no early'`.\n\nii.\n```python\nout[:, 2, :] = (tr['early_lick'][keep] == 'early').astype(np.int64)[:, None]\n```\n\niii. The trials table explicitly flags early licking.\n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Early lick*?\n\ni. Mapped to 0 (no early lick) or 1 (early lick) via boolean comparison. Per-trial value repeated across all 80 time bins.\n\nii.\n```python\nout[:, 2, :] = (tr['early_lick'][keep] == 'early').astype(np.int64)[:, None]\n```\n\niii. Simple binary encoding. Equivalent to the reference's dictionary-based mapping.\n\n## 8-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Tongue y-position* derived from?\n\ni. From `acquisition/BehavioralTimeSeries/Camera0_side_TongueTracking`, which contains `(n_frames, 3)` data = (tongue_x, tongue_y, tongue_likelihood) with corresponding timestamps. Column 1 (tongue_y) is the position; column 2 (likelihood) determines visibility.\n\nii.\n```python\ntt = f['acquisition/BehavioralTimeSeries/Camera0_side_TongueTracking']\ndata = tt['data']\nts = tt['timestamps'][:]\ny = data[:, 1]\nlik = data[:, 2]\n```\n\niii. This is the only tongue measurement in the NWB files. The tracking runs at ~300 Hz.\n\n## 8-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Tongue y-position*?\n\ni. Three steps:\n\n1. Frames with `likelihood <= 0.9` are marked as not visible (the AI uses 0.9 as the DLC default cut-off, vs the reference's 0.5).\n2. Per trial, visible frames within the [-2.5, 1.5] window are averaged into 50 ms bins using `np.bincount`.\n3. Per-session 40th and 60th percentiles are computed over all visible bin-mean values across all trials of the session.\n\nii.\n```python\nTONGUE_LIKELIHOOD_THRESHOLD = 0.9\n# ...\nvis = lik > TONGUE_LIKELIHOOD_THRESHOLD\n# bin within each trial window:\nb = np.floor((rel[v] - OFF_START) / BIN_SIZE).astype(np.int64)\ncounts = np.bincount(b, minlength=N_BINS)\nsums = np.bincount(b, weights=y[sl][v], minlength=N_BINS)\ny_bin[i, nz] = sums[nz] / counts[nz]\n\n# Percentiles over trial-window bin means:\ndef discretize_tongue(y_bin):\n    vals = y_bin[visible]\n    p40, p60 = np.percentile(vals, [TONGUE_PCTL_LOW, TONGUE_PCTL_HIGH])\n    code = np.where(y_bin < p40, 0, np.where(y_bin > p60, 2, 1))\n    out[visible] = code[visible]\n```\n\niii. The AI chose 0.9 as the threshold, noting the bimodal distribution makes it insensitive. The percentiles are taken over per-trial visible bin means (not the entire session's frames), which differs from the reference's session-wide binning approach.\n\n## 8-c. How is `output` *Tongue y-position* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Discretized into 4 classes:\n- 0: y < 40th percentile\n- 1: 40th percentile <= y <= 60th percentile\n- 2: y > 60th percentile\n- 3: not visible (no visible frame in the bin)\n\nii.\n```python\ncode = np.where(y_bin < p40, 0, np.where(y_bin > p60, 2, 1))\nout[visible] = code[visible]\n# non-visible bins default to 3\nout = np.full(y_bin.shape, 3, dtype=np.int64)\n```\n\niii. The boundary condition at exactly p60 assigns class 1 (the reference's `np.digitize` would assign class 2), but this affects negligible numbers of data points.\n\n## 8-d. How is `output` *Tongue y-position* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Camera timestamps are on the same session-absolute clock as the spikes and go cues. For each trial, frames within `[go + OFF_START, go + OFF_END)` are found via `searchsorted` on the camera timestamps, then assigned to bins by their offset from the trial window start using the same bin grid as the neural data.\n\nii.\n```python\nlo = np.searchsorted(ts, go_times + OFF_START)\nhi = np.searchsorted(ts, go_times + OFF_END)\nfor i in range(ntrials):\n    rel = ts[sl] - go_times[i]\n    b = np.floor((rel[v] - OFF_START) / BIN_SIZE).astype(np.int64)\n    np.clip(b, 0, N_BINS - 1, out=b)\n```\n\niii. Same go-cue-relative grid as the neural data ensures bin k of the tongue output covers the same interval as bin k of the firing rates.\n\n## 9. How are minor mistakes in the data, e.g. missing data, handled?\n\ni. Several cases:\n\n- **Session with no good units** (1 session): excluded by `session_passes` check for `ngood == 0`.\n- **Sessions not meeting behavioral criteria** (23 sessions): excluded by performance/correct-count criteria.\n- **Trials without spike coverage**: excluded via `units/obs_intervals` matching.\n- **Auto-water and free-water trials**: excluded as behaviorally uninformative.\n- **Silent trials** (no neuron fires): dropped post-binning (1 trial dataset-wide).\n- **Tongue not visible**: bins with no visible frame get class 3 (\"not visible\").\n- **Trial with no preceding tone onset**: falls back to `go - 1.85` (never triggered).\n\nii.\n```python\n# Session with no good units:\nelif ngood == 0:\n    reason = 'no units passed quality control'\n# Silent trials:\nsilent = fr.sum(axis=(1, 2)) == 0\nif n_silent:\n    keep[kept_positions[silent]] = False\n# Tongue fallback:\ntone = np.where(np.isnan(tone), go - 1.85, tone)\n```\n\niii. The AI documented each edge case in CONVERSION_NOTES.md and verified handling through sanity checks.\n\n## 10-a. What are the most time-consuming steps of the code?\n\ni. According to the AI's timing data: unit loading (reading spike times from HDF5) takes 0.06-0.6 s per session, spike binning takes 0.1-0.8 s per session, and inputs/outputs (including video reading) take 0.16-0.5 s per session. Total per session is 0.2-2 s. With 24 workers, full conversion takes ~22 s wall clock for 150 sessions. Pickling the output takes additional time for the ~10 GB file.\n\nii. N/A (timing is diagnostic output, not in the conversion logic itself)\n\niii. The AI measured and documented timing at each step and determined that the total was well within the 15-minute budget without needing further optimization.\n\n## 10-b. What loops in the code could have been vectorized to improve efficiency?\n\ni. Two loops remain:\n1. The per-unit loop in `bin_spikes` that runs one `np.searchsorted` per unit (all trials vectorized).\n2. The per-trial loop in `bin_tongue` that processes one trial's frames at a time.\n\nii.\n```python\n# Per-unit loop (trials vectorized):\nfor i, st in enumerate(spikes):\n    pos = np.searchsorted(st, edges).reshape(ntrials, N_BINS + 1)\n    fr[:, i, :] = np.diff(pos, axis=1)\n\n# Per-trial tongue loop:\nfor i in range(ntrials):\n    sl = slice(lo[i], hi[i])\n    # ... bincount per trial\n```\n\niii. The per-unit loop cannot be eliminated because each unit has a different number of spikes (ragged storage). The per-trial tongue loop could theoretically be vectorized with global bin indexing but is not a measurable fraction of runtime.\n\n## 10-c. What processing does the code repeat multiple times?\n\ni. The AI's two-pass approach reads the trials table from each NWB file twice: once during session selection (pass 1) and once during full conversion (pass 2). This doubles the I/O for the trials table but is a small fraction of total time.\n\nii.\n```python\n# Pass 1:\nfor fp in files:\n    with h5py.File(fp, 'r') as f:\n        tr = read_trial_table(f)\n        ok, perf, ncl, ncr, reason = session_passes(f, tr)\n\n# Pass 2:\ndef process_session(filepath, ...):\n    with h5py.File(filepath, 'r') as f:\n        tr = read_trial_table(f)\n        # ... full processing\n```\n\niii. The two-pass design avoids loading expensive spike/video data for sessions that will be excluded, but repeats the cheap trials-table read.\n\n## 10-d. What unnecessary processing does the code do that is discarded in downstream analyses?\n\ni. Several computations are performed that may not be needed downstream:\n\n1. **Session performance calculation** (`session_performance`) and the session selection criteria — these filter 24 sessions that the reference solution keeps.\n2. **Allen CCF ontology loading and structure-graph traversal** for brain region assignment — the reference uses simple string parsing instead.\n3. **CCF coordinate extraction** from the electrode table for the ALM carve-out.\n4. **Extensive diagnostic metadata** (timing info, excluded session details, tongue percentile values per session).\n\nii.\n```python\n# Allen ontology loading (unnecessary if using simple region labels):\ndef load_ontology(path=ONTOLOGY_PATH):\n    root = json.load(open(path))['msg'][0]\n    # ... recursive tree walk\n\n# Session performance (unnecessary if not filtering):\ndef session_performance(tr):\n    control = ((tr['early_lick'] == 'no early') & ...)\n    return float((tr['outcome'][control] == 'hit').sum() / responded.sum())\n```\n\niii. The brain region assignment using the Allen CCF ontology is more complex than needed but produces coarser (14 vs ~267) region labels that match the data paper's Fig. 1J grouping. The session filtering reduces the dataset from 173 to 150 sessions, discarding usable data.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
